**PM review policy (Brent, 2026-07-29, standing):** a finding in reviewed code is fixed in a remediation round before the chunk seals — "later-phase gate" / "open item" filings are NOT available for known defects. Chunk verdicts are binary: REVISE (new prompt to the executor) or sealed-and-ready for the phase pipeline.

**Z0B false start (process record, 2026-07-29):** the Z0B-2 prompt was mis-dispatched before Z0B-1 existed; the executor's STEP-0-style verification caught it and stopped clean (zero commits, zero changes, verdicts properly withheld — report in chat). Salvaged facts: local S3 multipart IS available (CLI 2.75.0 emits `[storage.s3_protocol] enabled = true`), Docker 29.2.1 healthy, `supabase/config.toml` is a 36-byte stub, storage container stopped. PM process fix now standing: ONE dispatchable prompt per handoff; every prompt opens with a STEP 0 identity/precondition self-check.

**Gate G1 = FAIL (recorded 2026-07-29, owner Brent):** FNE's Zoom account is **Pro** — below the Business/Education/API/Enterprise + ≥100-licenses entitlement for disclaimer text customization and consent reporting (§20 KB0068402). Consequences per §12 stand permanently: link-out-mode recording stays DISABLED; recording works only in SDK mode via the platform's pre-join consent capture; an unidentified participant in a recorded meeting makes that recording ineligible for transfer; Z0B-2's G2 check is expected to confirm FAIL. Do not chase the entitlement — the plan's consent architecture was designed for this outcome. Zoom Sales question stays open ONLY for pool-host sizing/pricing (Z6, §16).

## **PHASE Z1b: ✅ DONE (2026-08-03).**

**Merged to `main` as `97c36cc`** — a **merge-commit** ([PR #26](https://github.com/brentcurtis76/fne-lms/pull/26), MERGED) on Brent's explicit instruction, executed by the PM per the merge-authority policy. **310 branch commits preserved**; the PM verified after the fact that every ledger-cited SHA sampled across all chunks and rounds remains reachable on `main` — the squash-would-erase-history concern is closed, not just avoided.

**Pre-merge absorption** (`b4c3287`): 171 commits of parallel INSPIRA/T2/B1a work absorbed from `origin/main`, **zero conflicts**; the two overlap files unioned exactly as predicted (`ci.yml` keeps BOTH our Gate-3 queue-proof step and their seeded-Supabase e2e topology; `package.json` keeps `test:queue` alongside their new scripts). One operational note for future absorptions: **the merge brought new dependencies, so `tsc` vanished until `npm ci` ran** — always reinstall after absorbing a busy main. PROJECT_STATE phase-close flip in `a3f6e26`.

**Verification on the MERGED tree, all by the PM**: type-check/lint clean · **3992/3992 tests in 253 files** (our 3789 + the absorbed suites, zero interference) · build OK · **pgTAP 171/171 across 7 suites** on a from-scratch `db reset` (our 6 zoom migrations + main's new pasantias migration coexisting; the zoom suites' 139 all still hold) · **§17 queue proof green** (40 jobs, two concurrent loops, exactly-once 21/19) · scanner replication **CLEAN** (187 identifier values + 6 credentials, zero hits in tree and history) · **CI 8/8 at the merged head** including the union e2e job.

**Phase totals**: 4 build chunks + 2 PM remediation rounds + 9 Sol remediation rounds across **10 review rounds** (findings 6→4→3→1→2→2→2→3→4 items→APPROVE). Every finding remediated in its own round and independently verified by the PM. **Eleven explicit PM concessions**, each producing a recorded discipline change: binary chunk verdicts; identity-over-existence resolution; markers decided before anything that can overwrite them; test doubles read, not counted; guard commands never piped through `tail`. Artifacts: `fase-3-review-request.md` (executor), `fase-3-pm-dossier.md` (PM, §7b–§7j with errata), `fase-3-review-verdict.md` (Sol, all 10 rounds + triage).

**Post-merge state**: `ZOOM_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TOKEN` + `CRON_SECRET` were set in Vercel (fne-lms, Production) BEFORE the merge, so the merge's own deploy booted configured. **Production deploy of `97c36cc` succeeded and the PM SMOKE-TESTED the live endpoint (2026-08-03)**: on the canonical public domain `https://www.nuevaeducacion.org` (Brent, 2026-08-03 — the org-controlled domain; `nuevaeducacion.org` apex and the platform-generated `fne-lms.vercel.app` alias serve the SAME deployment and were both verified, but the `.vercel.app` name derives from the Vercel project name and would break on a project rename, so provider callbacks use the owned domain) a GET returns **405** (our method gate — and critically NOT the 404 unconfigured gate, which PROVES the secret env var loaded) and an unsigned POST returns **401 `{"error":"Unauthorized"}`** (our signature gate, our JSON). **TRAP FOUND AND AVOIDED — record it for every future webhook/callback URL in this project**: the per-deployment URL (`fne-<hash>-brent-curtis-projects.vercel.app`) sits behind **Vercel Deployment Protection** (`vercel_auth_enabled: true`) and answers 401 with Vercel's SSO payload — indistinguishable at a glance from a signature failure, and it would have made Zoom's CRC challenge fail for a reason nobody would have guessed. Provider callbacks must always target a STABLE domain. **Marketplace repoint DONE (Brent, 2026-08-03)** — endpoint `https://www.nuevaeducacion.org/api/zoom/webhook`, CRC validated and saved.

**⚠️ PHASE-CLOSE DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED THE SAME DAY — the checklist gap that caused it is the lesson.** The post-merge checklist this ledger carried (Marketplace repoint + two Vercel env vars) **never included applying the migrations to production**, and nothing in ten review rounds caught it: every gate ran against LOCAL Postgres (`supabase db reset`), CI included, so a green phase proved nothing about production's schema. Discovered only because the PM queried production to confirm the first webhook delivery and found `zoom_internal` absent — the last applied migration was `20260720134519`, from July 20.

**The severe part was NOT Zoom.** `createReservation` (`lib/services/hour-tracking.ts:326`, existing production code that Z1b-1 extended) inserts `contract_hours_ledger.planned_minutes_snapshot`; the column did not exist, so the insert failed and `pages/api/sessions/[id]/approve.ts:88` turned it into a hard 400 — **session approval was broken in production from the moment the merge deployed** (bulk-approve identically). Failure occurred before any status change, so no half-approved sessions and no orphaned ledger rows; the endpoint refused cleanly. Zoom-side impact was minor by comparison (real events 500'd and were dropped after Zoom's retries — all for meetings the platform does not manage, since nothing is provisioned until Z2; per-minute cron logged a failure per tick; CRC re-validation was unaffected because it is answered before any DB access).

**Repair (Brent-authorized, 2026-08-03):** 7 pending migrations applied to production in version order — the 6 Zoom ones plus the INSPIRA track's `20260731140500_add_pasantias_leads`, which was equally unapplied and whose deployed code had the same latent exposure. Each file wrapped BEGIN/COMMIT with its `schema_migrations` row in the same transaction (all verified free of CONCURRENTLY/VACUUM). The PM wrote and audited the script; **the agent's own execution was correctly blocked by the permission classifier as a production DB write, and Brent ran it** — the human-hand-on-production rule working as designed.

**PM verification of PRODUCTION after the repair (read-only, independent of Brent's report):** `planned_minutes_snapshot` present ✅ · `zoom_internal` = **7 tables, 9 functions** ✅ · `session_meetings_public` present with **RLS enabled and 3 policies** ✅ · `zoom_meetings_host_no_overlap` EXCLUDE constraint present ✅ · all 7 versions recorded in `schema_migrations` ✅ · **§6 lockdown INTACT IN PRODUCTION**: anon and authenticated have NO schema USAGE, **zero** table grants, **zero** function EXECUTE; service_role has USAGE and EXECUTE on all 9 ✅. Runtime tables all at zero rows, as expected pre-Z2.

**Regression CONFIRMED CLOSED without seeding production data (2026-08-03).** No sessions were pending approval (the platform is pre-Z2), so instead of writing test data the PM probed the API layer read-only with the publishable key: selecting `planned_minutes_snapshot` returns a PERMISSION error (RLS stopping an anonymous read — i.e. the column resolved and execution began), while a deliberately fake column returns `42703 column does not exist`. The negative control proves the probe can fail; the real column does not. The API layer therefore knows the column, and the exact failure mode that broke approval (`column does not exist` / stale PostgREST schema cache) cannot recur. The approval path was healthy before this phase and Z1b's only change to it was this one inserted field, so column resolution IS the delta.

**STANDING RULE ADDED — every future phase with migrations:** "apply migrations to production" is a REQUIRED post-merge checklist item, and phase close is not complete until the PM has verified the production schema directly (not just local/CI green). Local gates prove correctness; they say nothing about deployment. Z1c/Z2 dispatch prompts must carry this. (Zoom fires its CRC challenge on save; if it 401s, the route logs which of the five checks failed — Z1b-3's contingency). Until the repoint the webhook simply receives nothing; the two crons run and no-op on the empty queue. Open product call, any time: `hibrida` sessions get Zoom meetings (currently yes; one-line revert at `PROVISION_ELIGIBLE_MODALITIES`).

**Next up:** **Z1c — Synthetic tenant + CI e2e** (§15: `scripts/seed-e2e-zoom.js` with a local-only guard; blocking mock-mode specs — join-authz persona matrix, disclosure regressions, iCal content). Note the landscape shifted underneath it: main's T2 track already built a seeded-Supabase e2e topology (`scripts/ci/seed-e2e.mjs`, `e2e-mandatory.mjs`, `tests/e2e/helpers/auth.ts`), so **Z1c's first job is to reconcile its plan-era scope against what now exists** rather than build a parallel seeding path — the PM must dispatch that reconciliation explicitly. Then Z2 (link-mode session MVP, the first user-visible surface). **PM rotation (§0.1) is recommended HERE, at this phase boundary** — this ledger is the complete handoff. Interaction note: branch `chore/ts-tests` (separate track, unmerged) — either merge order works; overlap is `ci.yml` (different jobs) + `package.json` (one script line); the zoom branch is already clean under its gate · then Z1c → Z2 · parallel `user_roles_cache` session: still no PR — outside this ledger · ticket backlog (pre-existing, not Zoom): a test file under `pages/api/qa/__tests__/` ships as a production API route · machine housekeeping: the Z0B-2 throwaway Supabase stack (`z0b2spike`, 6 containers) may still be running — stop with `supabase stop --project-id z0b2spike` when convenient; worktrees `fne-lms-zoom-spike` (holds the gitignored `.env.spike.local` credentials + scanner captures — the PM scan replication needs them in place) and **`fne-zoom`** (Z1b, active — RENAMED from `fne-lms-zoom-core` on 2026-07-31; verify paths with `git worktree list`, judge mismatches by tree identity) are siblings of the repo; stale zero-byte lock `.git/refs/remotes/fne-old/main.lock` (Mar 2026) blocks an optional `git gc` — safe to `rm` when convenient · **PARALLEL ACTIVE TRACK (Brent, 2026-07-30): the main checkout (`/Users/brentcurtis/Documents/fne-lms-working`) is held by a separate conversation working the contracts bug fix on `feat/ctr-activate` (own commits, own `feat-ctr-activate-review-request.md`, outside this ledger).** Zoom sessions NEVER run git commands in that checkout — executors work only in the two zoom worktrees, and PM ledger commits to `main` go through a disposable DETACHED scratchpad worktree with push-by-SHA (`git worktree add --detach <scratchpad> origin/main` → edit → commit → `git push origin HEAD:main` → remove; UPDATED 2026-08-01 — the branch-checkout form broke the day the main checkout itself moved onto `main`), never by switching the main checkout's branch. The cause is `FIXTURE_KEYS = Object.keys(E2E_USERS) as FixtureKey[]`: the cast asserts a type the runtime value need not have, and the login block iterates the runtime value. Drift protection is therefore one-directional, and in the unprotected direction an untyped persona silently joins a blocking CI gate — the opposite of what the comment tells the next author. This is the Z1b-2-① / Sol-⑦ class (a false claim in a file whose entire purpose is to be the anti-drift tie), and it is comment-only to fix; assertions, fixtures and the persona set are untouchable in r1. |

**What the deploy ships to production:** ① the denial change — across six session GET consumers a caller denied a specific session now receives `404 'Sesión no encontrada'` instead of `403 'Acceso denegado'`; the one frontend consumer that branches on status (`pages/consultor/sessions/[id].tsx:162,167`) renders "Sesión no encontrada" for that case and keeps its `forbidden` branch reachable via the roleless 403. ② **`/api/sessions/[id]/attendees` goes live for the first time since `08bde65`** — it had returned 500 to every caller on every session since the feature shipped, so its disclosure branch had never once executed in production; the Z1c-2/Z1c-3 assertions are the first thing that has ever confirmed a non-privileged caller does not receive attendee addresses from it. ③ CI-environment variables only (`ZOOM_MODE=mock`, `CRON_SECRET`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` in the Gate-4 heredoc) — no production effect. **Nothing Zoom-facing changes: no meeting is provisioned until Z2.**

What merging DOES ship is one user-visible behaviour change: across six session GET consumers, a caller denied a specific session now receives `404 'Sesión no encontrada'` instead of `403 'Acceso denegado'`. The single frontend consumer that branches on status (`pages/consultor/sessions/[id].tsx:162,167`) shows "Sesión no encontrada" for that case; its `forbidden` branch stays reachable via the roleless 403. ## PHASE Z2 — Link-mode session MVP · IN PROGRESS (opened 2026-08-04)

**PM rotation executed at the Z1c→Z2 boundary, as §0.1 recommends.** This is a fresh PM session; state was reconstructed from `LEDGER.md` → `PLAN.md` → `reviews/` → `CLAUDE.md`, then verified against reality rather than trusted: `origin/main` at `513c75c`; PR #42 MERGED; Z1a/Z1b/Z1c all ✅ DONE; `lib/zoom/` carries 13 modules + 7 job files; 6 zoom-era migrations present; **`grep -rn is_zoom_managed` over `lib pages supabase scripts __tests__ tests` returns exactly three hits, all inside the `meeting-provision.ts:451-456` SEAM comment** — i.e. nothing enqueues `meeting_provision` today and no session is or can be managed. Z2's dependencies (Z1b + Z1c, §15) are both satisfied.

**Plan-doc drift corrected in passing (no plan change, docs only):** `PLAN.md` §15 and §0.2 point at `docs/plan/zoom/reviews/fase-<N>-review-request.md` while the Z1a–Z1c artifacts actually live there after the 2026-08-04 move (`513c75c`); the paths agree. Z2's review-request is **`fase-5`** (fase-1=Z1a, fase-2=Z0B, fase-3=Z1b, fase-4=Z1c).

**Chunking ruled for Z2** (§15 gives it 7–10 agent-days — far past one session). Five chunks, each a single executor session, in dependency order: **Z2-1** durable managed intent + provision-at-approve enqueue (this dispatch) · **Z2-2** join policy + join API + `/meet/session/[id]` SSR + JoinMeetingButton + the "Generar reunión Zoom" scheduler control · **Z2-3** reschedule sync + the atomic pre-execution reschedule→reservation+snapshot RPC + cancel/series/modality-flip `meeting_delete` · **Z2-4** notifications/reminders/iCal platform links + dual-zone wiring + dial-in display · **Z2-5** the §11 hours-consumer audit. Chunk boundaries are revisable between rounds; the ordering is not — Z2-1 must land first because every later chunk keys on `is_zoom_managed`.

**Standing rule carried into this phase (§0.1(d), the Z1b lesson): Z2-1 ADDS A MIGRATION.** Local and CI green prove code correctness and nothing about deployment. Phase close is not complete until the PM has verified the production schema directly with read-only queries, and "apply migrations to production" is a required post-merge checklist item — Brent-authorized, Brent-executed.

## **INFRASTRUCTURE EVENT — 2026-08-05: iCloud evicted every `~/Documents` checkout. ZERO WORK LOST.**

**What happened.** The Mac ran low on disk; macOS "Optimize Mac Storage" evicted local copies of files it judged unused, and it reached into the repo's `.git`. Every ZOOM checkout lived under `~/Documents`, which is iCloud-synced. **All of them were LINKED WORKTREES sharing one `.git` inside `fne-lms-working`** — so hollowing that single directory killed every worktree at once. Confirmed gone from disk: `fne-zoom`, `fne-zoom-sess`, `fne-e2e-tenant`, `fne-inspira`, `fne-lms-zoom-spike`; `fne-lms-pm` survives only as a bare `docs/` with no `.git` at all. **One shared `.git` under iCloud is a single point of failure for every workstream in the repo simultaneously** — that, not the disk pressure, is the lesson.

**Nothing was lost, and the PM verified that against GitHub rather than inferring it from a local repo it could no longer trust.** `gh api` on the remote: **`feat/zoom-sess` at `6c71eda`** carrying all 11 commits (Z2-1 r1 `f7a3df5` through Z2-2a r5 `6c71eda`), and **`main` carrying `4006cbd`**, so LEDGER.md and every prompt `Z2-r1`…`Z2-r6` are on GitHub. Every round of this phase was pushed before the next was dispatched, every chunk seal was verified at `git status --porcelain` = 0, and the r6 executor never reached the point of writing anything — it was still hunting for the plan in a hollowed checkout when it was stopped. **No uncommitted work existed to lose.**

**The recovery: ONE clone, not two.** `/Users/brentcurtis/dev/fne-lms` (created for the INSPIRA workstream) is outside iCloud, healthy, with `node_modules` installed and `.env.local` in place — and ZOOM and INSPIRA are **the same repo**, so that single clone serves both. A second clone would only cost another gigabyte on a volume at 94% (13 GB free). PM verified before adopting it: git operational, `origin/feat/zoom-sess` → `6c71eda`, `docs/plan/zoom/` complete including `prompts/Z2-r6.md`.

**Standing path rules, replacing everything earlier in this ledger that names a `~/Documents` path:**
- Repo: **`/Users/brentcurtis/dev/fne-lms`**. Executor worktrees: **under `/Users/brentcurtis/dev/wt/`**. **Never `~/Documents` — no exceptions, whatever an older ledger row or prompt says.**
- A fresh worktree has **no `node_modules` and no `.env.local`**; both must be provisioned before any gate runs. `.env.local` is gitignored, carries real keys, and is copied — never printed, never committed.
- The §0.1(e) rule stands and is now doubly earned: PM ledger commits go through the clone sitting on `main` or a detached scratchpad worktree with push-by-SHA, never by switching a checkout's branch.

**Documents corrected in this commit:** `PLAN.md` §0.1 and §0.2 — the PM-bootstrap and Sol-reviewer prompts both hardcoded the dead repo root and would have started the next PM and the independent reviewer in a directory that no longer exists. `prompts/Z2-r6.md` — re-pointed at the new clone, with the worktree-creation, `npm install` and `.env.local` steps written in, plus a head check (`rev-parse HEAD` must print `6c71eda…`) so a mis-created worktree stops the round instead of silently producing a diff against the wrong base. **`prompts/Z2-r1.md`…`Z2-r5.md` were deliberately NOT rewritten**: those rounds are executed and sealed, and their prompts are the historical record of what was actually dispatched. Rewriting them would falsify the trail this ledger exists to preserve.

**Phase state is unchanged by this event: Z2-1 and Z2-2a sealed (2 of 5 chunks), branch head `6c71eda`, 4350 tests / 266 files, pgTAP 338/8. Z2-2b (round r6) is dispatched and awaiting a fresh executor.**

Executor's own caveat adopted: what the test demonstrates is PostgreSQL's statement-level rollback of a single RPC call — which IS the property §11 asks for and is exactly what three route-level statements would not give — and a reviewer should read it as "one statement, therefore atomic", not as an explicit transaction block. **Chunk Z2-3a is NOT sealed. The chunk closes the last structural gap in the phase: after Z2-1/2a/2b/3a a session can be marked managed, provisioned, joined and re-costed — **but nothing ever tells Zoom**, so a rescheduled session keeps its original Zoom time and a cancelled session leaves a live meeting on a booked host forever. Scope: `meeting_sync` + `meeting_delete` handlers, both registered, and enqueues at the four lifecycle points (reschedule via PUT and via edit-request approve → sync; single cancel, series cancel, and the `online`/`hibrida`→`presencial` modality flip → delete). **Anchors verified against `334de02a`**: `registry.ts:67-80` (and the `runner.ts` rule that an unregistered type fails terminally); `api.ts:124-142` — **`patchMeeting` and `deleteMeeting` already exist**, `PatchMeetingInput` at `:87-93`; `fake.ts:211-225` — both already implemented, so mock mode needs no change; the projection RPC at `20260731120000_zoom_provision_rpcs.sql:179` whose status map the PM read directly (`provisioned→scheduled`, `started→live`, `ended→ended`, **`cancelled→cancelled`, `deleted→cancelled`**, else no publish) — which is what makes a deleted meeting answer **410** at the Z2-2a join endpoint; `[id]/cancel.ts` (`executeCancellation` at `:107`, session→`'cancelada'` at `:165-166`) and `series/[groupId]/cancel.ts`. **PM rulings inlined:** ① dedupe keys — `meeting_sync` keys on the TARGET schedule (`…:<starts_at_iso>:<duration>`) so a double-submit of one reschedule collapses to `'duplicate'` while a genuine later reschedule mints a fresh job, whereas `meeting_delete` keys statically per surface, since a surface is deleted once and never un-deleted; ② **a Zoom 404 on DELETE is SUCCESS** — the meeting is gone, which is the desired state, and retrying it to dead-letter would leave the projection permanently stale; ③ **a Zoom 404 on SYNC is a NON-RETRYABLE FAILURE, not success** — the meeting we believe we own does not exist, and silently re-creating it here would defeat the §9 host reservation by inventing a second creator; ④ no user action ever fails because of Zoom (§8, the rule Z2-1 established at approve) — enqueue after the state change commits, byte-identical responses in all outcomes; ⑤ the cancellation-clause flow (`executeCancellation`/`evaluateCancellationClause`/`calculateNoticeHours`, the `devuelta`/`penalizada` rows) is read-only to this chunk — the enqueue goes BESIDE it, never into it; ⑥ **re-check eligibility on claim, not just at enqueue** — the queue is at-least-once and a job may run hours later, so `meeting_sync` must refuse a since-cancelled session; ⑦ use the projection RPC, never a hand-written upsert, because its monotonic guard is load-bearing when an out-of-order webhook races a job; ⑧ **no new migration — proposing one is a stop-and-report**, since Z2-3a's is still unverified against production and a second unapplied migration would compound precisely the failure that broke session approval in Z1b. **Two process corrections carried into this prompt:** the ghost-round lesson is stated up front — **the report IS the deliverable**, a round that pushes silently is invisible to the ledger and cost a full extra round to reconstruct; and the DB gate is handled honestly — Docker's engine is down on this host, so the prompt requires `test:db` be attempted and reported as NOT RUN with the real error if it fails, explicitly forbidding the executor from adopting the earlier session's unreproduced `Files=9, Tests=374` as its own. Test doubles must be filter-aware, citing the Z2-2a probe where table-name-keyed doubles left a security filter unguarded at 39/39 green. Fail-on-old is available and required for [A7] — all four routes exist and enqueue nothing today. Baselines to meet or exceed: 4391 tests / 270 files. |

| Z2-5b | Z2 | `feat/zoom-sess` | — | 🔵 DISPATCHED 2026-08-05 | Prompt at `docs/plan/zoom/prompts/Z2-r12.md`. **The last build chunk of phase Z2** — §11's hours retarget, landing on the query r11 repaired. The audit half (r10's 15-site inventory) and the repair half (r11) are both done and are not redone here. **THE CENTRAL RULING, and the PM found the trap by reading the code rather than assuming**: `executeCancellation` (`hour-tracking.ts:470-481`) updates **only** `status`, `cancellation_clause`, `cancellation_reason`, `admin_override*` and `updated_*` — **it never changes `hours`**. So a `devuelta` row still holds the full originally-reserved amount even though those hours were RETURNED to the school, and a naive `SUM(hours)` would overstate what the school was charged. The distinction lives entirely in `status`. Hence the split ruling: **per-session display uses `hours` verbatim** (the status chip is already rendered beside it, so the number keeps its meaning and the change is behaviour-preserving), while **aggregates count `consumida` + `penalizada` only**, excluding `reservada` (not yet consumed) and `devuelta` (returned) — which is also the closest honest analogue of what the old field approximated, since `actual_duration_minutes` was only ever written at finalize. All four statuses must be asserted in both consumers. **Ruling on the no-ledger-row fallback, now concrete rather than left open as in r10**: fall back to `scheduled_duration_minutes` — **behaviour-preserving** for legacy and never-approved sessions (`actual_duration_minutes` was only ever a copy of the scheduled value) while corrective for ledgered ones, documented at the helper and tested in both consumers, because a report that quietly under-reports is worse than one that errors. **Ruling withdrawn and corrected in the prompt**: r10's ruling 2 required fixing a UI label rendering `total_hours_actual`; PM verified there is none (`reports.tsx:57` declares it, `:541` renders only `total_hours_scheduled`), so the prompt says so explicitly rather than leaving a fresh executor to rediscover it. Other rulings carried: `effective_minutes` does not exist and the helper reads `hours` behind a named Z7 SEAM (the `is_zoom_managed` device, which Z2-1 closed cleanly); keep the `total_hours_actual` response key since `reports.tsx` declares it and this is not a frontend refactor; `admin_override` rows flow through unchanged; **no migration — proposing one is a stop-and-report**; and **the two pre-existing defects r11 surfaced are explicitly NOT to be fixed here** (the `bucketError` `continue` at `:139-142` and `SESSION_STATUS_FALLBACK`'s three phantom statuses) — both await a separate PM ruling and touching either is scope creep. Tests build on r11's suite, which already executes `fetchSchoolReportData` for real against a schema-faithful double, so the retarget can be asserted end to end; analytics' `actual_duration_minutes` fixtures at `:77,88` must become ledger rows. Fail-on-old required for both consumers. **The prompt carries an explicit conflict clause** — if any two criteria conflict, STOP and report FINDINGS — because three of this phase's four correct executor refusals were drafting errors in prompts like this one. Baselines to meet or exceed: 4486 tests / 275 files. |

## ⛔ SCOPE FINDING — 2026-08-06: **Z2-4 was never dispatched, and the chunk denominator quietly dropped it.**

**Found by the incoming PM while reconciling the chunk map against `PLAN.md` §15, and it is BLOCKING for the phase, not for any round.** The Z2 chunking ruled at the top of this section names **five** chunks, of which **Z2-4 — "notifications/reminders/iCal platform links + dual-zone (`hora Chile` inputs + Madrid preview) wiring; dial-in display"** is one. It has **no dispatch row, no prompt, no verdict, and no deferral ruling** anywhere in this ledger — the only two occurrences of the string `Z2-4` in the whole file are the original chunking ruling and an r8 open item that names it as the owner of a live defect. Meanwhile the denominators drifted: `4 of 5` → `4 of 6` → `5 of 6` → `6 of 7`, and the r10 and r12 dispatch rows each call Z2-5 "**the last build chunk of phase Z2**". Counting the chunks actually executed — Z2-1, 2a, 2b, 3a, 3b, 5a, 5b — gives exactly 7, which is 5 chunks plus three splits **minus Z2-4**. The chunk was lost, not deferred.

**PM-verified against the branch, not inferred:** `git diff --stat main...feat/zoom-sess` matched against `notif|ical|remind|dial|zone` returns **nothing** — not one notification, iCal, reminder, dual-zone or dial-in file has been touched anywhere on `feat/zoom-sess`.

**What is actually still missing, after checking what earlier phases already delivered** (Z1a/Z1b shipped more of this row than the row's wording suggests — the platform-link half is done, PM-verified at `33d0bf73`, `a50d9d9e`, `61aa77bb` on `main`):

1. **The three lifecycle notifications do not exist.** `session_created`, `session_rescheduled` and `session_cancelled` are declared in `lib/types/consultor-sessions.types.ts:26,32,33` and **have no config in `NOTIFICATION_EVENTS` and no emitter anywhere** (PM grep across `lib/` and `pages/`). The registry at `lib/notificationEvents.ts:199-501` carries only the edit-request trio and the two reminders. So **a reschedule notifies nobody** — this is exactly the r8 open item "notifications/iCal still describe the OLD time after a reschedule (Z2-4)", and Z2-3b made it sharper by giving reschedule a real Zoom-side effect that participants are never told about.
2. **iCal cannot update an already-imported event.** `lib/utils/session-ical.ts` emits no `SEQUENCE` property (PM grep: zero occurrences), so a calendar client that imported the .ics before a reschedule keeps the old time permanently, regardless of what any notification says.
3. **Dial-in display has nothing behind it** — no `dial`/`pstn` column in either Zoom migration, nothing in `lib/zoom/db-types.ts`, no UI. §7's outage-fallback rationale (`PLAN.md:187`) depends on it. This needs a schema decision and a Zoom-response capture, i.e. a migration.
4. **Dual-zone is partially delivered**: `lib/utils/session-timezone.ts` exists and is consumed by `pages/meet/session/[id].tsx`, `admin/sessions/[id].tsx`, `consultor/sessions/[id].tsx`. What §15 asks for that is unverified is the **scheduler input** side ("hora Chile" inputs + Madrid preview).

**Ruling: Z2-4 is real, in scope, unamended in the plan, and splits — it is four concerns, not one.** Sized against §1.3 it is three chunks: **Z2-4a** the lifecycle notifications (item 1, no migration), dispatched below as r14; **Z2-4b** the iCal `SEQUENCE` fix (item 2, no migration, small); **Z2-4c** dial-in capture and display plus the dual-zone scheduler audit (items 3–4, carries a migration). 4b and 4c are scoped after r14 lands. **Phase Z2 is NOT build-complete and is NOT ready for the dossier or for Sol.** Corrected count: **7 of 10 chunks sealed** (Z2-1, 2a, 2b, 3a, 3b, 5a, 5b), Z2-4a in flight, Z2-4b and Z2-4c unscoped.

## ✅ **DB GATE CLEARED 2026-08-06 — the four-round dark stretch is over.**

**Brent restarted the machine and brought Docker back; the PM ran the gate immediately at the branch head `3685644c`, before dispatching anything further.** Sequence, all by the PM: daemon confirmed (`server 29.2.1`); the local Supabase stack was **already running** and — because worktrees of one repo share a project id — could have been holding another branch's schema, so it was **checked before being trusted rather than after**: `npx supabase migration list --local` returns **all 12 migrations with `local` and `remote` matching**, including both Zoom migrations (`20260804120000` zoom_managed_intent, `20260805120000` reschedule_hours_rpc). No drift, no local-only file, nothing remote-only — so **no `db reset` was needed**, which also avoided disrupting any concurrent workstream sharing that stack. Then `npm run test:db` → **`Files=9, Tests=374, All tests successful, Result: PASS`, exit 0**, with `011-zoom-public-rls.sql` and `012-reschedule-hours-rpc.sql` both green.

**What this settles:** the phase's longest-standing gap, open since the r13 verdict. Chunks Z2-4a, Z2-4b and the r13 hours fix all shipped without the DB gate; none of them touched SQL, and the gate now confirms the tree they landed on is clean. **All five gates are green at `3685644c`.**

**What it does NOT settle, and the §0.1(d) rule stands unchanged:** this is a LOCAL Postgres. Z2 carries two migrations that have **not been applied to production**, and local green proves nothing about deployment — the lesson Z1b paid for with a broken session-approval in prod despite ten green review rounds. Phase close still requires the PM to verify the PRODUCTION schema with read-only queries, and "apply migrations to production" remains a Brent-authorized, Brent-executed post-merge step. **Carry the r13 residual-risk count with it** (`consultor_sessions` with `status='completada'` and no `contract_hours_ledger` row) — one authorized read-only session can answer both.

**Dispatch consequence:** chunk **Z2-4d (dial-in) is UNBLOCKED** and becomes r18. Round r17 (Z2-4c, dual-zone) stays as dispatched rather than being reordered ahead of it — it was already committed, it needs no database, and reordering would buy nothing but a renumbering. `prompts/Z2-r17.md` was amended in this commit: its §0 now states the gate is green and makes `test:db` **mandatory rather than conditional**, its §4 no longer gives a stale reason for holding dial-in, and **[D8] is now a five-gate criterion with `Files=9, Tests=374, PASS` as the baseline — r17 changes no SQL, so an identical result is required and any deviation is a stop-and-report.**

A 7th parameter is a NEW function in Postgres, so "persist on the same write" meant either a `DROP` — forbidden RED-tier by `CLAUDE.md`, with no precedent outside the baseline — or a defaulted overload, which **breaks the existing DB gate**: `002-zoom-internal-isolation.sql` calls both functions POSITIONALLY with six arguments (`:411,431,447,461,477,493`) and asserts EXECUTE grants against the LITERAL 6-arg signature (`:98-114`), so an overload would make the calls ambiguous (42725) while the grant assertions kept passing against a stale function silently writing NULL forever. So: add `dial_in_numbers jsonb` and set it inside both RPCs via `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` at the **unchanged 6-argument signature** — additive, no DROP, no overload, every pgTAP signature assertion still valid, and it matches the file's own documented idiom (its header reads **"AMENDED IN PLACE"**, `:3`). **Option B (generated column) REJECTED** — the round's reason stands (no persistence step left to mutate, so the probe could not be honest) plus a generated column could never hold a backfilled value. **Option C (DROP + 7 params) REJECTED** — it is what the prompt literally asked for, and asking for it was the PM's error: it breaks a RED-tier rule for marginal explicitness and carries a production-ordering hazard the local gate cannot see, on a project that has already shipped unapplied migrations once. **Consequences carried into r19 so they need not be inferred: `meeting-provision.ts` is NOT modified at all this round** (the round read "do not change its control flow" correctly; under Option A the strongest reading is no change), **S3 is withdrawn, and the mutation probe moves to the RPC** — and is now required to hit ONE of the two functions only, so the tests must distinguish the two provisioning paths. **The wire-shape dependency the round flagged is ruled, with its limit stated:** Zoom reports the numbers as `settings.global_dial_in_numbers`; that is from Zoom's API documentation, **not an observed tenant response**, and **CI can never retire the risk** because CI runs `ZOOM_MODE=mock` against the fake this chunk writes. ## ⛔ **SOL VERDICT ON PHASE Z2: `REQUEST CHANGES` — twelve items (2026-08-06).**

**The phase is NOT approved and NOT mergeable.** Sol reviewed the dossier + review-request + the 75-file diff and returned twelve remediation items. **The PM verified the three highest-severity claims against the code before chunking anything, and all three hold.**

**① `DROP` in `20260805120000_reschedule_hours_rpc.sql:50` — CONFIRMED, and it is the PM's error twice over.** The migration carries `ALTER TABLE public.session_activity_log DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS session_activity_log_action_check`, re-adds a widened 17-value allowlist at `:53-66`, validates at `:68-69` — and its own header at `:25` declares "ADDITIVE ONLY: no DROP TABLE, no TRUNCATE, no destructive ALTER", arguing at `:41-44` that a CHECK widening is "mechanically DROP + ADD" and therefore exempt. **The argument loses**: `CLAUDE.md` states the rule with no mechanism exemption, and a hard rule that yields to a good argument is not a hard rule. **The PM approved this in the Z2-3a round without grepping the migration for `DROP`, and then in r18 told a different executor that a `DROP FUNCTION` was RED-tier forbidden — the same rule, opposite rulings, two rounds apart.** Sol caught the inconsistency the PM created.

**⑧ `bucketError` swallowed — CONFIRMED, and the PM had it in hand twice and did not rule it.** `lib/services/school-hours-report.ts:143` fetches `get_bucket_summary`'s error and the surrounding loop continues, so a failed ledger query reports as "this bucket has no hours". r11 logged it, r13 and r19 carried it forward as "unruled", and **the PM never ruled it** — leaving a fail-open money path in a report Sol then found. This is the same defect class r11 was dispatched to repair one chunk earlier, which makes leaving it doubly indefensible.

**⑥ Managed sessions get no platform link — CONFIRMED, and it silently guts two sealed chunks.** Every link site gates on `meeting_link` being truthy: `session-lifecycle-notifications.ts:281`, `session-reminders.ts:109`, `ical.ts:216` and the other two iCal endpoints. But **Z2-1 forces `meeting_link` to NULL on the managed transition by design** — the real join URL lives in `zoom_internal`, never in `consultor_sessions`. So for exactly the sessions this phase exists to serve, notifications, reminders and every .ics emit **no link at all**. Chunks Z2-4a and Z2-4b were built to tell people about a session and, for managed sessions, tell them nothing actionable.

**Chunking ruled — seven remediation rounds, grouped by concern rather than by Sol's numbering:** **Z2-S1 (r21, dispatched)** items 1+2, the reschedule transaction · **Z2-S2** item 3, availability aggregation + `get_bucket_summary` · **Z2-S3** items 4+9, join authorization from authoritative source status in both the route and SSR · **Z2-S4** item 5, `meeting_provision`/`meeting_delete` serialization per surface · **Z2-S5** items 6+7, platform links for managed sessions + the WorkspaceSessionsTab control · **Z2-S6** items 8+10, fail-closed ledger read + a migration-level backfill test · **Z2-S7** items 11+12a, `PROJECT_STATE.md` and the final gate run. **Item 12b — the staging checklist against a real audio-plan Zoom tenant (approve→provision→join per persona, immediate cancellation denial, Zoom-side deletion, and the observed `global_dial_in_numbers` shape) — is NOT executor work.** It needs a real Zoom account and real credentials; it is Brent's, and it is the only thing that can retire the standing "no real tenant has ever returned dial-in numbers to this code" risk.

| Z2-S2 · r22 | Z2 | `feat/zoom-sess` | — | 🔵 DISPATCHED 2026-08-06 | Prompt at `docs/plan/zoom/prompts/Z2-r22.md`. Fork point `374cdbac`. **Sol item 3, and the PM confirmed it in the SQL before dispatching — it is the most consequential finding of the review and it is NOT a Z2 bug.** `public.get_bucket_summary` (`00000000000000_baseline.sql:2781-2820`) builds `effective_allocations`, then `LEFT JOIN contract_hours_ledger chl ON chl.allocation_id = ea.id` and aggregates `SUM(ea.allocated_hours)` **over the joined set**. The join fans out: one allocation with N ledger rows becomes N rows, so the allocation's hours are summed **N times**. `allocated_hours`, `available_hours` and `annex_hours` are all inflated by the ledger's row count — a 100-hour allocation with three sessions reports **300 allocated** — and **the error grows every time a session is booked, always in the direction of telling a school it has more hours than it bought.** `reserved_hours` and `consumed_hours` are correct, since they sum `chl.hours` and each ledger row appears once. **This function lives in the BASELINE and feeds the admin and equipo_directivo hour dashboards — it is WRONG IN PRODUCTION TODAY, independent of this branch.** Z2 only surfaced it because the reschedule RPC pinned its own availability expression against it (`20260805120000:163-171`), which is the second copy Sol wants reconciled. **Rulings:** aggregate allocations and ledger separately then combine — **never** `SUM(DISTINCT …)`, which would silently swallow two legitimately-equal allocations, and `[J2]` exists to catch exactly that non-fix; `CREATE OR REPLACE` at the identical signature with **no `DROP`** (repeating r21's error here would be worse than the original); the return shape is fixed because every dashboard consumer reads it by name; the three baseline EXECUTE grants stay untouched; and the reschedule RPC should **call** the repaired function rather than keep a hand-maintained copy. **`[J1]`** is the fan-out case with explicit numbers, **`[J4]`** the boundary over-budget case Sol asked for, **`[J8]`** a probe that restores the fan-out. **Ruling 5 flags the human consequence: dashboards will show FEWER available hours after this lands, because today's number is inflated — that is the fix working**, and the round must report the blast radius so Brent gets an accurate note. |

| Z2-S4 · r24 | Z2 | `feat/zoom-sess` | — | 🔵 DISPATCHED 2026-08-06 | Prompt at `docs/plan/zoom/prompts/Z2-r24.md`. Fork point `f8bf88ab`. **Sol item 5, dispatched in parallel with the item-9 decision because it is fully independent of it.** `claim_zoom_jobs` leases with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, which guarantees two tickers never lease the same job ROW and guarantees **nothing about two different jobs for the same surface** — and Vercel crons overlap by design, as that file's own header states. **The interleaving:** the provisioner passes its eligibility check while the session is `programada`; the session is then cancelled, releasing the reservation and enqueueing `meeting_delete`; the delete runs, finds no `zoom_meeting_number` because nothing has been created yet, and completes; the provisioner resumes and creates and persists the meeting. **Final state: a live Zoom meeting attached to a cancelled session, with the delete already run and not coming back.** Ruled: coordinate the two per surface, mechanism the executor's choice (advisory lock, conflicting-type-excluding claim, or re-check plus compensation) — **the acceptance test is the END STATE, not the mechanism**: no Zoom orphan, no active reservation, cancelled projection, under either interleaving order. Constraints: `meeting-provision.ts`'s CAS and error taxonomy were settled over four earlier review rounds and may gain a coordination point but not be restructured; **no `DROP`, no overload, no signature change** — the rule already broken once this phase and repaired; and **a compensation path is acceptable but silence is not** — if a meeting can be created and then deleted, that must be an explicit, durable, observable step. `[L1]` requires the interleaving to be **driven deterministically**, not run hopefully; `[L4]` requires no deadlock or livelock; `[L6]` requires a probe that shows the orphan actually appearing. |

## 📘 **PLAN AMENDED 2026-08-06 — §5's join-policy line, by owner decision. Sol item 9 resolved.**

**Brent ruled after the PM laid out the trade-off in plain terms; the PM did NOT adopt this unilaterally.** `PLAN.md` §5 read *"`authorizeMeetingJoin()` … used by both the join API and the `/meet` pages' getServerSideProps"*, and Sol's item 9 quoted it correctly. Round r23 refused to implement it and was right — see the r23 verdict row for the verified evidence.

**What the PM got wrong, and it is worth stating precisely because it shaped two rounds:** the plan's intent — *one authorization decision governs joining* — is correct. The PM aimed it at the wrong lever, trying to satisfy it by narrowing **page visibility** instead of by gating the **capability**. That would have re-tiered four roles as a side effect of a security fix, which is exactly what a security fix must not do.

**The defect the amendment actually names, which nobody had stated before:** `pages/meet/session/[id].tsx` offers **two ways into a meeting and guards only one**. The Zoom join affordance runs through `authorizeMeetingJoin`; a **raw pasted `meeting_link`** — supported today for `google_meet`, `teams` and `otro`, with a link field live in the scheduler — is simply printed on the page, so page visibility alone decides who can use it. **Same capability, two rules, chosen by whichever provider the scheduler happened to pick.** The PM verified the provider enum and the create form before recommending this.

**Brent's initial position was that the concern was moot because the platform has never been used and everything will be new at launch.** The PM agreed on the narrow point — there is no accumulated data to leak — but verified and pushed back on the general one: **pasted links are not a legacy artifact**, they are a live supported path that produces the same exposure the first time anyone schedules a non-Zoom meeting. The two non-data arguments (roles losing access; a mandatory spec encoding a rule) stand regardless. Brent then asked for the best-practice answer rather than a menu, and ruled for it.

**The amendment, recorded in `PLAN.md` §5 with its full rationale:** page visibility keeps `canViewSession`, consistent with every other session surface; `authorizeMeetingJoin()` governs the join **capability** wherever it appears on that page — Zoom affordance **and** raw pasted link; the §14 kill switch is enforced in `/meet` SSR as §14 already required. Neither §5's *"no page props carries a Zoom secret"* guarantee nor §14 is weakened.

**Scope note for the round that implements it:** gating the pasted link is **slightly wider than Sol asked for** and touches behaviour that predates this phase. The PM proposed it explicitly as the coherent fix and Brent approved it knowing that. It is a pre-existing hole the Zoom work walked into, not scope creep — but it is a deliberate, owner-approved widening and Sol should see it labelled as such.

**Sequencing: r24 (Sol item 5) is IN FLIGHT.** The item-9 implementation becomes **r25**, and its prompt will be written once r24 lands, so its fork point and gate baselines are real rather than guessed — the standard this phase has held to for twenty-four rounds.

| Z2-S4 · r24 verdict | Z2 | `feat/zoom-sess` | `f2c22a74` | ✅ APPROVED 2026-08-06 — **Sol item 5 CLOSED** | **PM re-ran all five gates at `f2c22a74`**: type-check 0 · lint 0 · **4659 passed / 281 files** (+11) · build 0 · **`test:db` Files=11, Tests=464, PASS** — unchanged, correct for a round adding no SQL. **THE MECHANISM CHOICE IS THE BEST-ARGUED OF THE PHASE, and the PM accepts the reasoning as given:** an advisory lock cannot be held across this handler's PostgREST calls (no stable database session), so it would degrade into a second lock table with its own lease and expiry races; a claim that excludes conflicting job types coordinates the CLAIMS, not the WORK — a provisioner whose lease expires mid-`createMeeting` walks into the identical window — and it would land in the one RPC four review rounds have hardened. **The re-check sits inside the process holding the unpersisted meeting, which is the only place that can still undo it.** The window-closure argument is complete and checks out: the reservation INSERT precedes `createMeeting`, so a delete finding NO row must have read before that INSERT and its job is only claimable after the cancel commits — putting the cancel ahead of re-check #1; a delete after the CAS finds the number and removes the meeting normally; the only ordering left is a cancel committing between the two, which is coordination point #2. **PM ran its own mutation probe and deliberately a SURGICAL one.** The round neutralised BOTH coordination points (6 of 11 new tests died, with the orphan printed). The PM disabled **only point 2** — the CAS-miss retirement — leaving the post-create re-check intact: **exactly ONE test failed, the residual-window case.** That proves the suite distinguishes the two points rather than merely detecting that coordination exists at all. Blob `417f8ee8…` identical after revert, tree clean. **THE DEVIATION IS ACCEPTED, AND ITS OWN FRAMING IS CORRECTED — it is a gap-fill, not the reversal the round called it.** The round said a previous round "settled the other way", citing the module rule at `:19`: *"A row that already carries a `zoom_meeting_number` is left alone… freeing it would let a second meeting be booked onto an occupied host."* **That rule governs a row WITH a number. The new compensation governs a NUMBERLESS reservation whose meeting exists only in the job's post-create checkpoint** — a case the old rule never addressed, and where refusing and walking away left the meeting standing. **The old rule's intent is not weakened but preserved**: when compensation FAILS the row keeps its `pending` status with the number in `evidence`, so the §9 EXCLUDE constraint goes on blocking that host and `meeting_delete` refuses to release it (`delete_compensation_parked`, on the `ambiguous_create_outcome` precedent). Loud, not silent — which is what `[L5]` demanded and what the round delivered. **Assumptions accepted**: treating a compensated provision as a COMPLETION rather than a failure, following the three existing `superseded` precedents — the world is correct afterwards and there is nothing for a human to do; and `error` deliberately NOT counting as a "retired" status, since it is the provisioner's own definite-failure record rather than a decision about the surface. **PM RULING on the round's open question — it goes to Z2-S6, not here.** `meeting_delete` still fails terminally under `no_meeting_row` in the named interleaving: **a red dead-lettered job for a race the system now handles correctly.** That is operational noise that trains people to ignore dead-letters, which is worse than the noise itself. Ruled: **a dead-letter must mean a human needs to act**; when the end state is already correct it must not dead-letter — but a blanket "no row ⇒ completed" would hide a genuine anomaly, so the distinction must be made rather than the check removed. Bundled with Sol items 8/10 in Z2-S6, the error-signalling round. **Open items carried:** a lease lost between `createMeeting` and the re-check still strands a meeting — the module's pre-existing residual, narrowed for the cancelled-session case but not for crashes in general; the compensation is itself three calls (Zoom DELETE, release, republish) and a crash between them leaves a self-healing stale projection or a parked-looking row whose meeting is already gone; modality flips are covered by construction since the whole gate re-runs, but only `cancelada` is asserted. **Sol items 1–5 CLOSED. Seven remain.** |

## ⛔ **BLOCKED ON AN OWNER DECISION — the amended §5 collides with a MANDATORY e2e spec. Found before dispatching, not by an executor.**

**The PM was about to dispatch the item-9 implementation and checked the mandatory spec first — the exact check whose absence caused the r23 stop.** It fails the same way, one layer down.

`PLAN.md` §5 as amended this morning says `authorizeMeetingJoin()` governs the join **capability** wherever it appears on `/meet/session/[id]` — the Zoom affordance **and the raw pasted `meeting_link`**. **`tests/e2e/zoom-join-authz.spec.ts:70-79` asserts the opposite for the view-only tier**: `VIEWER_PERSONAS` reach `meet-join-link` and the test asserts its **`href` equals the raw `LINKED.meetingLink`**, with a comment explaining the href is deliberately the assertion rather than the affordance. That spec is MANDATORY (`scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs:23`).

**So the spec documents the very inconsistency the amendment names.** For a Zoom-managed session, "view-only" means *sees the page, receives no credentials*. For a hand-pasted-link session, "view-only" means *receives the link* — which is the whole capability. **The tier name is accurate for one provider and a misnomer for the other**, and the PM's recommendation to Brent, which Brent approved, cannot be implemented without inverting those assertions.

**The PM should have checked this before recommending. It is the ninth PM error of the phase** and the second of exactly this shape — recommending a change without first reading the mandatory spec that encodes the current rule.

**The decision is Brent's and it is narrow:** *for a session whose meeting is a hand-pasted link, should a view-only persona still get that link?* Yes ⇒ the amendment's clause 2 is scoped to Zoom-managed sessions only, the inconsistency is accepted and documented, and the spec stands. No ⇒ clause 2 stands as written, those assertions invert, and view-only genuinely means view-only for every provider.

**Nothing is dispatched on item 9 until this is answered.** The §14 kill switch — the half of item 9 with no persona consequence — is carried into the round that implements whichever answer comes back, so `/meet` SSR is touched once rather than twice.

**OWNER RULING 2026-08-06 on the mandatory-spec collision: MAKE IT CONSISTENT.** Brent ruled directly, declining to spend further time on the tier question: view-only means the same thing for every provider. **`PLAN.md` §5's amendment stands exactly as written — no further plan change is needed** — and `tests/e2e/zoom-join-authz.spec.ts:70-79`'s view-only assertions invert. A persona who may view but may not join gets **no join affordance and no raw link**, whether the meeting is Zoom-managed or a hand-pasted link. The denied and privileged tiers are untouched, and **no page-visibility tier moves** — that is `[M3]`, which exists to prove it.

## ⛔ **SOL RE-REVIEW (round 2) ON PHASE Z2: `REQUEST CHANGES` — narrowly. 2026-08-07.**

**Nine of twelve items closed and closed well; Sol calls the remediation "the strongest this workstream has produced". Four MAJOR and six MINOR remain. Sol re-ran ALL SIX gates end to end and every figure matches the PM's record exactly** — type-check clean · lint clean · **4726/283** · build OK · **`test:db` Files=11, Tests=466** after a fresh reset · **mandatory e2e 88 passed, 7 specs, zero retries, guard OK**. `.env.local` never written.

**Both owner rulings were challenged on the merits and UPHELD.** Ruling A (the §5 amendment): *"The original line aimed 'one authorization decision' at the wrong lever… The amendment fixes the actual defect — two join paths under two rules — without moving any page tier."* Ruling B (inverted view-only assertions): *"Correct, and the replacement assertions are strictly stronger than what they replaced."* The §14 scope call was upheld too. **The r23 refusal is vindicated in the reviewer's own words.**

**THE PM VERIFIED ALL FOUR MAJORS AGAINST THE CODE BEFORE DISPATCHING. EVERY ONE HOLDS.**

**M1 — the item-5 residual, and the chain is exact.** Reservation INSERT → `createMeeting` succeeds → checkpoint lands → the attempt dies **retryably** → the session is cancelled → `meeting_delete` claims the **numberless** row and **skips the Zoom call entirely** (`meeting-delete.ts:529`, PM-read), marks it `deleted` and **clears `last_error`** (`:547`), completing **green** → the provision retry's `strandedCheckpoint` requires `ZOOM_MEETING_ACTIVE_STATUSES.includes(held.status)` (`meeting-provision.ts:2131-2134`, PM-read) and the row is now `deleted`, so it is **false** → terminal `session_ineligible`, *a reason that reads as handled*. **End state: cancelled session, LIVE Zoom meeting, two green jobs, no loud marker** — the item-5 harm verbatim, in the crash-shaped case the third compensation site existed to close. **It also disproves r27's premise that r24's compensation is unconditional — which the PM's own dossier §A4 item 6 had flagged as unproven. The "not verified" section did exactly the job it was written for.**

**M2 — three more swallowed reads, in the function r27 repaired.** `school-hours-report.ts` destructures only `data` on `schools` (`:85`), `clientes` (`:94`) and `contratos` (`:105`), PM-verified. A failed `clientes` read coalesces to `[]` and returns `{ programs: [] }` — **a 200 whose whole-school report shows zero contracts and zero hours, indistinguishable from a school with nothing billed.** **The PM's r27 prompt quoted specific line anchors and the round stayed on them; that framing is the PM's error.**

**M3 — a failed read writes the wrong money status.** `hour-tracking.ts:426` swallows the `hour_types` read, so `modality` stays `'online'` and a **`presencial`** session cancelled 120 h out evaluates as clause 1 (`devuelta`, consultant unpaid) instead of clause 4 (`penalizada`, consultant paid). **PM RULING, which Sol explicitly reserved to the PM: FIX NOW.** Pre-existing is not a defence when the harm is a wrong billing status persisted to a ledger row, and r27 set the precedent of fixing the class. `getAvailableHours` (m2) is display-only and goes to the backlog.

**M4 — and this is the one that forced the verdict.** The branch carries **SEVEN** migrations (PM-verified by `git diff --name-only`); `PROJECT_STATE.md` says **"cuatro migraciones"** in the phase summary, the deploy-blocking debt and the phase-close checklist. **An operator following that checklist applies four and misses `apply_session_reschedule` and both bucket-summary functions — production then calls RPCs that do not exist and every duration-relevant reschedule 500s.** That is **the Z1b closing defect re-armed by the very document that records the permanent rule against it** (`PROJECT_STATE.md:13-14`). Ruled: replace the count with an explicit **filename manifest** — a count drifts silently, a list of filenames cannot. **The PM's own dossier says "6" while enumerating seven (Sol's m5): the PM got the same number wrong, in the document written to be the reviewer's map.**

**MINORs ruled:** m1 (the compensation-loudness claim overclaims on one of three triggers) → qualify the text, do not restructure. m4 (edit-request path passes no `if_updated_at`, leaving the racy JS comparison while the pgTAP-proven guard goes unused) → **pass it; it is free**. m6 (five artifacts cite "recorded in `PLAN.md` §5" but the **branch copy** predates the amendment, which lives on `main`) → **do NOT merge `main` into the branch this round** — it would muddy the fix diff Sol re-reviews — **record the split in the review file instead.** m2, m3 and m5 are folded into R-B/R-D. Sol's own note that `'edit_approval_blocked'`'s audit insert **also** swallows its error is recorded with the standing item: same class, ticketed, and r21's discriminator pattern is the fix shape when it is ruled.

## ✅ **SOL VERDICT ON PHASE Z2: `APPROVE WITH NOTES` — 2026-08-07.**

**All four MAJOR findings verified closed by the reviewer**, each on its own terms: M1 — *"the real two-attempt/delete interleaving is reproduced, the retired row is compensable only with the matching checkpoint, and the deleting writer's row/projection are preserved"*; M2 — failed `schools`/`clientes`/`contratos` reads now fail loudly with legitimate-empty still distinct; M3 — the failed `hour_types` lookup aborts **before ledger writes**, preventing the wrong monetary classification; M4 — the seven migration files truthfully manifested. **m6 closed. m4 closed, and Sol went further than the PM did**: it examined the two overlapping concurrency guards the r29 round flagged and ruled them **complementary and correctly worded** — the old-value guard rejects an already-stale request, the transactional `updated_at` guard catches a race after the read, and their different 409 instructions are appropriate. That resolves an open item without a round. Sol re-ran every gate plus the RLS-disable guard and `git diff --check`; all clean.

**TWO MINORS REMAIN, AND THE PM RULES BOTH FIX-NOW.** §0.2 gives the PM this call and requires it recorded either way.

**MINOR 1 — the failed-compensation message can claim a host slot that is already free.** `holdsReservation` is captured **before** awaiting the Zoom DELETE, so a concurrent `meeting_delete` can retire the numberless row mid-flight; if the DELETE then fails, the operator copy says the reservation *"keeps blocking its host"* when it no longer does. The durable marker, the meeting number and the required manual action all stay correct, so this is triage accuracy rather than a compensation failure — **but it is the same principle this workstream already ruled on in r27**, when it refused to let `meeting_delete` dead-letter for a race the system handled: **a signal a human must act on has to be true, or it teaches people to distrust the channel.** Consistency decides it. Ruled to **Sol's state-neutral option** rather than a post-failure re-read: another round trip on an error path can be stale again by the time it lands, whereas copy that always names the meeting and the required action is true in every ordering.

**MINOR 2 — the numberless precondition has no negative control, and this one the PM found first.** The r29 test proves the positive case; **nothing proves that a retired row already carrying `zoom_meeting_number` must NOT enter the compensation branch.** The PM's r29 mutation — dropping `held.zoom_meeting_number === null` — passed **all 116** tests in that suite, and **Sol reproduced the same result independently**. The predicate is correct and the reviewer says so; what it guards is an **ownership boundary**: a numberless retired row is the one whose retirement *proves* `meeting_delete` never called Zoom, so this process still owns the meeting; a retired row **with** a number was retired by a writer that **did** call Zoom, and compensating it would issue a DELETE against a meeting another writer owns. **A boundary that took two rounds to establish and that nothing defends is worth one negative test**, and `[S4]` makes the round prove it by showing that exact mutation now fails.

**Neither MINOR blocks anything Brent needs to do**, and the staging run (12b) can proceed in parallel — it exercises no code this round touches.

## ⚠️ **BINDING PROCESS RULE ADOPTED 2026-08-08 (`main` @ `28979090`) — and it applies to Z2 at close.**

Another workstream's PM established, and recorded in `docs/plan/PLAN.md`'s Decision Log: **"every phase opens a PR before merge, and no phase closes on local gates alone."** The reason is mechanical — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` triggers on **`pull_request` and `push: [main]` only**, so **a branch pushed without a PR gets ZERO CI coverage**, and merging becomes the first time the six checks ever run — on the same push that auto-deploys. CI then *observes* the deploy instead of gating it. The corollary is explicit: **a PM may not write "CI gate N is the evidence" for a branch that has no PR.**

**What this means for Z2, stated plainly:** `feat/zoom-sess` has been pushed for thirty rounds and **has never had a single CI run.** Every gate figure in this ledger, in `fase-5-review-request.md` and in both dossier addenda is **local**: the PM's own five-gate runs, the executors', and Sol's independent re-runs. That is a great deal of evidence and it is all from one machine. The PM has never claimed otherwise — the dossier's identity row has said **"PR: NONE OPEN"** since it was written, and no artifact in this phase cites a CI run as evidence — but the rule now makes the gap a **close blocker** rather than a note.

**Added to Z2's close sequence, ahead of the merge:** open the PR for `feat/zoom-sess`, let the six checks run, and confirm them green **on CI** — not only locally. This is outward-facing and is **Brent's to do**. It sits alongside the two items already outstanding: **item 12b** (the staging run against a real audio-plan Zoom tenant) and the **seven-migration production apply and read-only verification** (§0.1(d)). None of the three can be satisfied by another executor round.

## 🔴 **PR #45 OPENED 2026-08-08 — AND CI CANNOT RUN: the PR is `CONFLICTING`. The rule earned its keep on day one.**

Brent opened **[PR #45](https://github.com/brentcurtis76/fne-lms/pull/45)** per the binding rule adopted the same day. **GitHub Actions did not start a single check**, and the reason is not a flake: `gh pr view` reports `mergeable: CONFLICTING`, `mergeStateStatus: DIRTY`, and GitHub does not run `pull_request` workflows when it cannot compute a merge commit. Vercel's preview built (it does not need one); **the six real gates never began.**

**`main` has moved 136 commits since `feat/zoom-sess` forked.** PM-computed with `git merge-tree --write-tree` — a read-only merge simulation that mutated nothing — the conflicts are exactly three files, and all three are **shared CI fixtures**:

```
scripts/ci/e2e-fixtures.json
scripts/ci/e2e-mandatory.mjs
scripts/ci/seed-e2e.mjs
```

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` auto-merges clean. **Both workstreams added to the same three files**: Z2's r28 added the managed-session fixture and registered `zoom-managed-join.spec.ts` as mandatory; the pasantías/A8 workstream added its admin-leads fixtures and registered `pasantias-leads-admin.spec.ts`.

**This is precisely the class of problem thirty rounds of local green could never surface**, and it appeared within minutes of the first PR. Two teams had been editing the same CI seed files for days with no way to notice.

**PM RULING — the resolution is a UNION, never a choice**, and the risk is asymmetric: **`e2e-mandatory.mjs` is the list of specs that MUST run**, built so a green gate cannot mean "the specs were skipped". A resolution keeping only one side leaves a file that still parses, still runs and **still reports green** while another team's required coverage has silently vanished — the exact failure that guard exists to prevent, reintroduced by a merge. `[T2]` therefore demands both spec names printed, and `[T5]` demands the whole list actually run, so the union is proved by execution rather than by reading.

**`[T6]` protects the approved work**: the Z2 feature diff against `4be9f7d6` must be **empty** across `lib/zoom/**`, the meeting utils, the four services, `pages/api/meet/**`, `pages/meet/**` and `supabase/migrations/**`. A merge round may resolve conflicts; it may not move the diff Sol approved.

**The merge also closes Sol's m6** by bringing `PLAN.md` §5's owner-authored amendment (`50c28855`) onto the branch, where six shipped artifacts already cite it.
